# Task-type: code-review-acceptance

Anchor: `.hopper/tasks/code-review-acceptance.md::root`

## Purpose

Verify a change against its stated acceptance criteria. Review only — no edits.

## Input shape

- The task spec section from `.hopper/handoffs/leader-tasklist.md` (matched by task ID)
- Acceptance criteria (prefer machine-checkable: a runnable command or grep per criterion)
- Positive scope (files allowed) and negative scope (files that must not change)
- Budget: time and vendor-cost ceiling

## Output shape (output.md)

The output should contain, in this order:

- **Summary**: what was delivered, in two to four sentences
- **Files touched**: paths with a one-line rationale each (or "none")
- **Acceptance verification (N/N)**: each criterion with evidence (command output, file:line, grep match)
- **Decisions / deviations**: judgment calls or scope changes (or "none")
- **Open questions**: list, or "none"
- **Verdict**: PASS | PASS_WITH_NOTE | REWORK | FAIL
- **Next recommendation**: what should happen next

## Notes

This frame describes the SHAPE of the work and the expected output, not an
identity to adopt. The vendor CLI brings its own behavior; the frame only states
what the protocol expects back.

# T-062 · harnessloop v0.13.0 / TH-0008 对抗审

## Summary

已对 `harnessloop` commit `d6234cf` 的五项 Rule B 放宽逐项做反例、边界 fixture 与独立 mutation；基线 `validate.py` 全绿，历史语料的 `1054/532 → 900/235` 也可精确复算。四条假阴性守卫都有至少一条会随对应 mutation 翻红，嵌套 submodule 的 decoy 修法同样有效；但实现仍会稳定放过断链符号链接、带尾斜杠的文件引用、被噪声剪枝掩盖的多义后缀、索引后删除的文件，以及由 `.gitmodules`/`../` 逃出项目的路径。上述均触及本次最高优先级的“真悬空不得假绿”，故 TH-0008 暂不能判 fixed。

## Files touched

- `.hopper/handoffs/T-062-output.md`：本只读对抗审交付物。
- 被评审源码：none；所有 mutation 均在 detached 临时 worktree 中执行、逐次还原，最终 worktree 已删除。

## Acceptance verification (5/5)

### 1. 五项放宽逐条证伪 — **完成；②③存在必须修复的假阴性，④是明确但很宽的未覆盖面**

1. **尾部 locator 剥离**：普通不存在文件仍会报。实现先试原文，再仅对剥离结果做 `.exists()`，最后才进入后缀回退（`verify_protocol.py:250-269,490-499`）；基线 fixture 的 `rounds/.../does-not-exist.md:999` 仍为 dangling。将 `:494` 临时改成无条件 `resolved = True` 后，`validate.py` 退出 1，唯一失败为 `verify still catches a dangling citation even with a numeric locator suffix`。这一项自身守卫有效；但剥离后仍会继承后缀索引的断链 symlink 漏报。
2. **嵌套 submodule base**：真实 `path = kernels/vendorlib` 正向通过；把 `submodule_roots()` 临时恢复成 `if not rel or "/" in rel` 后，带同后缀 decoy 的端到端断言单独失败，证明 `validate.py:930-948` 的 decoy 已彻底隔离后缀回退。反例是解析器对 path 没有合法性/项目内 containment 检查（`verify_protocol.py:335-346`）：`.gitmodules` 写 `path = ../outside` 时，外部 sibling 会成为 citation base，`pkg/ghost.py` 被放过；绝对 path 或指向项目外的目录 symlink 同理。
3. **后缀唯一回退**：核心正向和零命中/多命中/段边界 helper 守卫都能翻红，但实现不满足 docstring 所称“每个实际存在文件”“全项目树恰好唯一”（`:52-68`）。可复现的漏报见第 3、4 项：断链 symlink、尾斜杠、剪枝后伪唯一、陈旧快照均返回 resolved。另有方案本身无法消除的语义碰撞：引用 `mistyped/config.yaml` 只要无关路径 `vendor/mistyped/config.yaml` 是全树唯一后缀，就会被视为有效；算法证明的是“某个唯一后缀存在”，不是“它就是评审者原本想引用的文件”。
4. **`~/` 与 `/` 绝对路径豁免**：`pathish_citations()` 对 `~/definitely-missing/evidence.md`、`/definitely-missing/evidence.md` 均返回 `[]`（`verify_protocol.py:234-247,293-306`），因此不存在的外部证据会静默退出 Rule B，且不计入 `citations_checked`。这与 `:70-81` 的 docstring 一致，属明确的协议边界而非实现偷渡，但它确实是无牙的假阴性面；此外 `C:/...` 未被识别为绝对路径，在 Windows 上仍可能重现此次声称消除的真实主机文件系统依赖。
5. **外部设计 wiki 刻意不修**：定性声明诚实——相对写法如 `kernel/d1-kernelport-spec-v3-5.md` 仍报 dangling，而 `~/.llm-wiki/...` 写法被明确列为“never verified”（`:75-81`），没有宣称已验证外部 wiki。定量 `133/235` 无法按当前固定语料复现：同一 `900/235` 残留中，有 **145/235** 条剥离 locator 后直接存在于 `~/.llm-wiki/agent-app-design/`（144 个文件引用、1 个目录引用）；`133` 缺少可执行分类口径。

### 2. 四条假阴性守卫与嵌套 submodule mutation — **5 次均按要求 FAIL，恢复后 PASS**

所有 mutation 均在 `/tmp/t062-harnessloop-review.*/repo` 的 detached `d6234cf` worktree 内执行，命令均为 `HARNESSLOOP_SKIP_CLAUDE=1 python3 scripts/validate.py`：

| Mutation | 退出码与关键证据 |
|---|---|
| 零命中也接受：`:410` 改为 `return len(matches) >= 0` | exit 1；拼错 basename helper 与端到端 `SKIILL.md` 均 FAIL（共 10 条受影响断言） |
| 多义也接受：`:410` 改为 `return len(matches) >= 1` | exit 1；仅 `rejects an ambiguous suffix matching >=2 real files` FAIL |
| 放开单段：`:406` 改为 `len(parts) < 1` | exit 1；helper 的 bare `SKILL.md` 断言 FAIL |
| locator 一律豁免：`:494` 改为 `resolved = True` | exit 1；不存在文件带 numeric locator 的端到端断言 FAIL |
| 回退嵌套 submodule：`:342` 恢复 `or "/" in rel` | exit 1；仅 nested multi-segment submodule 断言 FAIL |

有一处测试隔离瑕疵：放开单段后，端到端 `agents/` 断言仍显示 `ok`，只有 helper 断言翻红。原因是 `build_suffix_index()` 只索引 `filenames`（`:377-379`），从不索引 `agents/` 目录；所以 `validate.py:1010-1013` 所称“即使更长 suffix 会命中”不成立，该端到端断言没有测试到 `len(parts) >= 2` 守卫。建议改成唯一存在的裸文件名 fixture。

还原证据：五次 mutation 后 `git status --short` 为空；再次运行 validate 得 `restored_exit=0` 与 `Plugin framework validation passed.`；随后临时 worktree 已删除。主工作区 `harnessloop` 源码始终 clean。

### 3. 段边界、归一化、大小写、symlink — **存在 3 类绕过**

对 `suffix_unique_match()` 的独立探针输出：

```text
pkg/real.md          -> True
x/../pkg/real.md     -> False
pkg//real.md         -> True
pkg/real.md/         -> True    # 错：实际对象是文件，带尾斜杠不应存在
PKG/real.md          -> False
pkg/Real.md          -> True
host exact case      -> True
host wrong case      -> True    # 本机卷大小写不敏感
```

- **尾斜杠漏报**：`:405` 用 `if p` 丢弃全部空段，令 `pkg/real.md/` 与 `pkg/real.md` 等价；端到端 fixture 中前者 `dangling_count=0`。
- **断链 symlink 漏报**：`os.walk()` 会把指向不存在目标的 file symlink 放进 `filenames`，`:377-379` 不做 `exists()/is_file()`；`deep/pkg/broken.md -> missing-target.md` 后引用 `pkg/broken.md`，端到端 `dangling_count=0`。
- **`../` 项目逃逸**：后缀 helper 自身不会归一化 `..`，但更早的 `(base / cited).exists()`（`:490`）会让 OS 归一化且未做 containment。项目 sibling 中存在 `outside/ghost.py` 时，引用 `../outside/ghost.py` 得 `dangling=[]`。这与 docstring 将 Rule B 定义为 project-relative 且需“exist in the project”（`:10-12`）矛盾。
- **大小写**：后缀 tuple 比较是大小写敏感的，未被字符串 `endswith` 绕过；但显式 base 的 `.exists()` 遵循宿主文件系统，本机错误大小写仍可通过、Linux 会失败，结果不具跨平台确定性。

### 4. 索引、噪声剪枝、运行确定性 — **基线性能稳定；快照与剪枝各有可复现漏报**

- 基线真实项目连续三次：exit 均 0，耗时 `0.54s / 0.46s / 0.45s`，JSON 三次字节一致；均为 `violations=0`、`rounds=14`、`rule_a_files=8`、`rule_b_files=3`、`citations=12`、`zero_inspected=9`。单次构建在静态工作区性能与确定性良好。
- **剪枝掩盖多义**：fixture 同时创建 `src/pkg/real.md` 与真实源码目录 `build/pkg/real.md`，引用 `pkg/real.md`。`:373-379` 在遍历时删除 `build`，索引只看到一个候选，端到端 `dangling_count=0`；这违反 `:65-67` 的“whole project tree / multiple still report”。硬编码“这些目录 never 被引用”（`:136-140`）没有协议依据。
- **陈旧索引漏报**：先 `build_suffix_index()`，再删除唯一的 `deep/pkg/gone.md`，随后 `suffix_unique_match("pkg/gone.md", index)` 仍为 `True`，同时 `target_exists=False`。索引只建一次（`:558-563`）且匹配时不重验存在性；并发删除或替换可导致同一输入随时序假绿。

最小反例的共同输出如下（fixture 全在系统临时目录，未写仓库）：

```text
trailing_slash_file       dangling_count=0
broken_symlink            dangling_count=0
noise_pruned_ambiguity    dangling_count=0
submodule_parent_escape   dangling_count=0
stale_index_after_delete  match_after_delete=True target_exists=False
```

核心 helper 可从仓库根直接复现：

```bash
python3 - <<'PY'
import importlib.util, shutil, tempfile
from pathlib import Path
s = Path("harnessloop/plugins/harnessloop/skills/harnessloop-loop/scripts/verify_protocol.py").resolve()
sp = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("vp", s)
vp = importlib.util.module_from_spec(sp); sp.loader.exec_module(vp)
r = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="t062-repro-"))
try:
    p = r / "plain"; (p / "deep/pkg").mkdir(parents=True)
    (p / "deep/pkg/real.md").write_text("x")
    print("trailing", vp.suffix_unique_match("pkg/real.md/", vp.build_suffix_index(p)))
    (p / "deep/pkg/broken.md").symlink_to("missing-target.md")
    print("broken", vp.suffix_unique_match("pkg/broken.md", vp.build_suffix_index(p)),
          (p / "deep/pkg/broken.md").exists())

    n = r / "noise"
    for rel in ("src/pkg/real.md", "build/pkg/real.md"):
        f = n / rel; f.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True); f.write_text("x")
    print("pruned-ambiguous", vp.suffix_unique_match("pkg/real.md", vp.build_suffix_index(n)))

    st = r / "stale"; (st / "deep/pkg").mkdir(parents=True)
    gone = st / "deep/pkg/gone.md"; gone.write_text("x")
    idx = vp.build_suffix_index(st); gone.unlink()
    print("stale", vp.suffix_unique_match("pkg/gone.md", idx), gone.exists())

    project = r / "project"; outside = r / "outside"; project.mkdir(); outside.mkdir()
    (project / ".gitmodules").write_text("[submodule \"x\"]\n\tpath = ../outside\n")
    print("escaped-root", [x.resolve() for x in vp.submodule_roots(project)])
finally:
    shutil.rmtree(r)
PY
```

### 5. 数量、基线与边界声明复算 — **主数字对齐；wiki 子分类不对齐**

- `HARNESSLOOP_SKIP_CLAUDE=1 python3 harnessloop/scripts/validate.py`：exit 0，132 项检查全绿（Claude strict validation 按要求 skip）。
- 以 61 个 tracked `T-*-output.md` 加 `git show HEAD:.hopper/handoffs/leader-tasklist.md` 作为 commit 当时的 62 文件语料，旧版 `d6234cf^` 与新版 `d6234cf` 分别得到：

```text
old  citations=1054 dangling=532
new  citations=900  dangling=235
```

- 当前工作树的 `leader-tasklist.md` 已追加 T-062 自身，直接扫描会变成 `905/236`；为避免把待审任务文本倒灌进基线，上述复算固定使用其 `HEAD` 内容。这是本审唯一语料假设。
- 在同一 235 条残留上检查 `~/.llm-wiki/agent-app-design/<stripped citation>`，直接存在数为 145，不是 133。定性“相对 wiki 路径仍未修、home-absolute wiki 路径则被排除而非验证”与 docstring 一致；仅量化分类需要补脚本/口径。

## Decisions / deviations

- Verdict 采用 **REWORK** 而非 PASS_WITH_NOTE：断链 symlink 与索引后删除都能把“此刻不存在的目标”判为 resolved，正中任务规定的最高风险；剪枝伪唯一和项目逃逸进一步表明问题并非只在极端竞态。
- 未修改任何被评审文件。为满足 mutation 要求，使用 detached 临时 worktree 而非主工作区；每次 mutation 后先还原，最终验证 clean 并删除 worktree。
- 除按 `HEAD` 固定已被当前 T-062 文本污染的 `leader-tasklist.md` 外，无 scope 或验法偏离。

## Open questions

- `build/dist/bin/obj/coverage` 等名称是否要被协议正式定义为“Rule B 后缀域之外”？若没有明确契约，不能以性能优化名义从唯一性计数中删除。
- 外部引用究竟要“显式 `verify:ignore` 并留审计痕迹”，还是允许 `~/`、`/` 自动静默排除？当前实现选择后者，覆盖率也看不到被排除数量。

## Verdict

# Task-type: code-review-adversarial

Anchor: `.hopper/tasks/code-review-adversarial.md::root`

## Purpose

Independently review a change, hunting for defects the author would miss. Review only — no edits.

## Input shape

- The task spec section from `.hopper/handoffs/leader-tasklist.md` (matched by task ID)
- Acceptance criteria (prefer machine-checkable: a runnable command or grep per criterion)
- Positive scope (files allowed) and negative scope (files that must not change)
- Budget: time and vendor-cost ceiling

## Output shape (output.md)

The output should contain, in this order:

- **Summary**: what was delivered, in two to four sentences
- **Files touched**: paths with a one-line rationale each (or "none")
- **Acceptance verification (N/N)**: each criterion with evidence (command output, file:line, grep match)
- **Decisions / deviations**: judgment calls or scope changes (or "none")
- **Open questions**: list, or "none"
- **Verdict**: PASS | PASS_WITH_NOTE | REWORK | FAIL
- **Next recommendation**: what should happen next

## Notes

This frame describes the SHAPE of the work and the expected output, not an
identity to adopt. The vendor CLI brings its own behavior; the frame only states
what the protocol expects back.

+  The index backing this fallback is built from the project's
+  git-tracked files (`git ls-files --recurse-submodules`, run from the
+  project root, when the project root is itself a git working-tree top
+  level) — including files inside nested submodules — falling back to
+  walking the filesystem (pruning `NOISE_DIR_NAMES`) only when the project

+  literally "the whole project tree": a generated or gitignored file that

+  named `build/`) is indexed and can correctly participate in an
+  ambiguity instead of being silently pruned out of consideration

+  when it is used, still prunes `NOISE_DIR_NAMES` and is subject to the
+  same pruning blind spot it always was.
+

+  when the matched filesystem entry is actually a directory (`is_dir()`),
+  never when it merely shares a path with an existing file of the same
+  name (TH-0008 REWORK: `trailing_slash_file` — `pkg/real.md/` must not
+  resolve against the file `pkg/real.md`). This directory semantics is
+  applied uniformly to explicit-base resolution and to the suffix
+  fallback.

+  absolute), or a Windows-style absolute path — a drive letter
+  (`C:/...`, written `C:\\...` before backslashes are normalized to `/`)

+  which already starts with `/` and so falls under the same check) — is

+  `scheme://` URL is exempt: it names a location outside the project tree,
+  and Rule B has no project-declared base to resolve it against —
+  inventing one (e.g. treating `/` as the OS root, or `C:/` as a Windows
+  drive root) would let the check silently walk outside the project, or
+  behave differently depending on which OS the check happens to run on.
+  This is a real, known gap: a review that cites a path inside an external
+  design wiki kept outside this project (e.g. under a user's

+  know where — or whether — that external tree exists. Closing that gap
+  requires a project to declare an additional resolution base for such
+  external stores; that is a separate, project-level protocol decision and
+  is out of scope here. Unlike before, this exemption is no longer silent:
+  every span skipped for this reason is counted in the `coverage` dict's

+  Two honesty notes on what this rule cannot mechanically guarantee even
+  after the above:
+
+  - Case sensitivity of the final existence check is whatever the host
+    filesystem gives it, not a Rule B decision: `Path.exists()` /
+    `is_dir()` are case-sensitive on a typical Linux filesystem and
+    case-insensitive (but case-preserving) by default on macOS and
+    Windows. A citation with the wrong case for a real file can therefore
+    pass or fail depending on which machine runs the check — this rule
+    does not normalize case itself and cannot make that determinism
+    project-wide.
+  - The suffix-unique fallback proves "exactly one indexed file has this
+    suffix", not "this is the file the reviewer meant to cite". A
+    reviewer who mistypes `mistyped/config.yaml` when they meant a
+    different file will still get a pass if `vendor/mistyped/config.yaml`
+    happens to be the tree's only file ending in `mistyped/config.yaml` —
+    the algorithm cannot distinguish a lucky coincidental match from the
+    intended one. This is inherent to any suffix-based fallback and is not
+    something this rework (or any purely mechanical suffix match) can
+    close; it is recorded here rather than fixed because fixing it would
+    require either dropping the fallback (reintroducing the false
+    positives it exists to remove) or requiring citations to be written

+def _git_tracked_index(project: Path) -> dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]] | None:
+    """Git-tracked-file index for `build_suffix_index`, or `None` if
+    `project` is not itself a git working-tree root (or `git` is
+    unavailable).
+
+    Only engages when `project` *is* the git top level — not merely inside
+    one — so a scratch directory nested under some ancestor repository
+    (e.g. a test fixture created under this repo's own working tree without
+    being `git add`ed) correctly falls back to the walk-based index instead
+    of silently seeing zero tracked files and treating every suffix lookup
+    as absent.
+
+    Uses `git ls-files --recurse-submodules`, which lists tracked files in
+    `project` and inside every nested submodule (any depth) without ever
+    walking into gitignored output (`node_modules/`, `dist/`, `build/`
+    artifacts, `.venv/`, ...) — so the resulting uniqueness universe is the
+    project's real tracked-source surface, not an approximation of it via a
+    hardcoded noise-directory denylist (see module docstring: "unique"
+    means unique among git-tracked files, not literally the whole tree).
+    """
+    try:
+        toplevel = subprocess.run(
+            ["git", "-C", str(project), "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel"],
+            capture_output=True,
+            text=True,
+            timeout=10,
+        )
+    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
+        return None
+    if toplevel.returncode != 0:
+        return None
+    top = toplevel.stdout.strip()
+    if not top:
+        return None
+    try:
+        if Path(top).resolve() != project.resolve():
+            return None
+    except OSError:
+        return None
+
+    try:
+        listed = subprocess.run(
+            ["git", "-C", str(project), "ls-files", "-z", "--recurse-submodules"],
+            capture_output=True,
+            timeout=60,
+        )
+    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
+        return None
+    if listed.returncode != 0:
+        return None
+
+    index: dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]] = {}
+    for raw in listed.stdout.split(b"\0"):
+        if not raw:
+            continue
+        try:
+            rel = raw.decode("utf-8")
+        except UnicodeDecodeError:
+            continue
+        parts = tuple(p for p in rel.split("/") if p)
+        if not parts:
+            continue
+        index.setdefault(parts[-1], []).append(parts)
+    return index
+

+def _resolve_in_project(base: Path, cited: str, project: Path) -> Path | None:
+    """Join `base` and `cited`, normalize, and return the result only if it
+    stays within `project` — otherwise `None`.
+
+    Guards against a citation containing `../` segments walking the join
+    outside the project tree, where `Path.exists()` would silently consult
+    the real host filesystem for an unrelated path that happens to share a
+    name with something the review meant to cite (TH-0008 REWORK:
+    `submodule_parent_escape` — the same containment discipline
+    `submodule_roots` applies to `.gitmodules` entries, applied here to
+    every citation resolution).
+    """
+    candidate = Path(os.path.normpath(str(base / cited)))
+    if not is_under(candidate, project):
+        return None
+    return candidate
+
+
+def _any_base_resolves(cited: str, bases: list[Path], project: Path, want_dir: bool) -> bool:
+    """True if `cited` resolves (containment-checked, directory-semantics-
+    aware) against any of `bases`."""
+    for base in bases:
+        resolved = _resolve_in_project(base, cited, project)
+        if resolved is not None and _exists_as(resolved, want_dir):
+            return True
+    return False

+        # fallback -- see build_suffix_index / _git_tracked_index docstrings. `git add`
+        # (not `commit`) is enough for `git ls-files` and needs no configured identity.
+        git_available = shutil.which("git") is not None
+        if git_available:
+            init = subprocess.run(["git", "init", "-q"], cwd=noise_root, capture_output=True)
+            add = subprocess.run(["git", "add", "-A"], cwd=noise_root, capture_output=True)
+            git_available = init.returncode == 0 and add.returncode == 0
+
+        if git_available:
+            violations, _coverage = verify_protocol.verify_project(noise_root)
+            check(
+                any("pkg/real.md" in v["detail"] for v in violations),
+                "verify (git-tracked index) reports the ambiguous `pkg/real.md` suffix as "
+                "dangling instead of resolving it via a noise-pruning false-uniqueness "
+                "(TH-0008 REWORK: noise_pruned_ambiguity, codex T-062)",
+            )
+
+            # Mutation control: reproduce the pre-REWORK walk-based index (NOISE_DIR_NAMES
+            # pruning, no git) directly and confirm it *would* have seen only one
+            # candidate -- proving the git-tracked-index switch, not something else, is
+            # what makes the assertion above hold.
+            pruned_index: dict[str, list[tuple]] = {}
+            for dirpath, dirnames, filenames in os.walk(noise_root):
+                dirnames[:] = [d for d in dirnames if d not in verify_protocol.NOISE_DIR_NAMES]
+                rel_dir = Path(dirpath).relative_to(noise_root)
+                dir_parts = () if str(rel_dir) == "." else rel_dir.parts
+                for filename in filenames:
+                    pruned_index.setdefault(filename, []).append(dir_parts + (filename,))
+            pruned_matches = [
+                c for c in pruned_index.get("real.md", [])
+                if len(c) >= 2 and c[-2:] == ("pkg", "real.md")
+            ]
+            check(
+                len(pruned_matches) == 1,
+                "mutation control: the walk-based NOISE_DIR_NAMES-pruned index sees only "
+                "one `pkg/real.md` candidate (proves the git-tracked-index switch, not "
+                "existence re-checking, is what surfaces this ambiguity)",
+            )
+        else:
+            print("  (skipped: git unavailable -- noise_pruned_ambiguity counterexample)")
+    finally:
+        shutil.rmtree(noise_root, ignore_errors=True)
+
+    # TH-0008 REWORK counterexample 5/5: submodule_parent_escape. A `.gitmodules`
+    # `path =` entry pointing outside the project root (`../outside`) must not become
+    # a citation resolution base, even though the sibling directory genuinely exists
+    # on the host filesystem. codex T-062 repro: dangling_count=0 pre-fix (the escaped
+    # base let `pkg/ghost.py` resolve).
+    escape_root = REPO_ROOT / ".tmp" / f"verify-fixture-escape-{uuid.uuid4().hex}"
+    escape_project = escape_root / "project"
+    escape_outside = escape_root / "outside"
+    round_dir6 = escape_project / ".harnessloop" / "goals" / "20260107-001-fixture" / "rounds" / "0001"
+    try:
+        (round_dir6 / "evidence").mkdir(parents=True)
+        (round_dir6 / "reviews").mkdir(parents=True)
+        (round_dir6 / "scope-lock.md").write_text(
+            "# Scope Lock\n\n## Allowed Changes\n\n"
+            "- Write evidence under `rounds/0001/evidence/`.\n"
+            "- Write reviews under `rounds/0001/reviews/`.\n",
+            encoding="utf-8",
+        )
+        (escape_outside / "pkg").mkdir(parents=True)
+        (escape_outside / "pkg" / "ghost.py").write_text("# outside the project\n", encoding="utf-8")
+
+        (escape_project / ".gitmodules").write_text(
+            '[submodule "escaped"]\n\tpath = ../outside\n\turl = https://example.invalid/escaped.git\n',
+            encoding="utf-8",
+        )
+
+        (round_dir6 / "reviews" / "escape.md").write_text(
+            "Citation that only exists via a .gitmodules path escaping the project root, "
+            "must NOT resolve:\n"
+            "- `pkg/ghost.py`\n"
+            "\n"
+            "Citation containing a literal `../` that would walk the *project root* base "
+            "itself outside the project tree, must also NOT resolve (this is the general "
+            "containment check in `_resolve_in_project`, independent of submodule_roots):\n"
+            "- `../outside/pkg/ghost.py`\n",
+            encoding="utf-8",
+        )
+
+        roots = verify_protocol.submodule_roots(escape_project)
+        check(
+            all(r.resolve() != escape_outside.resolve() for r in roots),
+            "submodule_roots rejects a .gitmodules `path =` entry that resolves outside "
+            "the project root (TH-0008 REWORK: submodule_parent_escape containment, "
+            "codex T-062)",
+        )
+
+        violations, _coverage = verify_protocol.verify_project(escape_project)
+        check(
+            any("pkg/ghost.py" in v["detail"] and "../outside" not in v["detail"] for v in violations),
+            "verify reports `pkg/ghost.py` as dangling instead of resolving it through an "
+            "escaped .gitmodules submodule base (TH-0008 REWORK: submodule_parent_escape, "
+            "codex T-062)",
+        )
+        check(
+            any("../outside/pkg/ghost.py" in v["detail"] for v in violations),
+            "verify reports a literal `../`-escaping citation as dangling instead of "
+            "resolving it against the project root's parent directory (TH-0008 REWORK: "
+            "general containment in _resolve_in_project, codex T-062)",
+        )
+
+        # Mutation control: without containment, the escaped path still genuinely
+        # holds pkg/ghost.py on disk -- proving the rejection above comes from the
+        # containment check, not from the file being absent.
+        check(
+            (escape_project / ".." / "outside" / "pkg" / "ghost.py").resolve().is_file(),
+            "mutation control: the escaped path still genuinely holds pkg/ghost.py on "
+            "disk (proves containment, not absence, is what the check above relies on)",
+        )
+    finally:
+        shutil.rmtree(escape_root, ignore_errors=True)

import importlib.util, json, os, tempfile, shutil
from pathlib import Path
script = Path('harnessloop/plugins/harnessloop/skills/harnessloop-loop/scripts/verify_protocol.py').resolve()
spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('vp', script)
vp = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec); spec.loader.exec_module(vp)
root = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='t062-adversarial-'))
results = {}

def fixture(name, citation, files=(), symlinks=(), gitmodules=None):
    p = root/name
    rd = p/'.harnessloop/goals/g/rounds/0001'
    (rd/'reviews').mkdir(parents=True)
    (rd/'evidence').mkdir()
    (rd/'scope-lock.md').write_text('# Scope Lock\\n\\n## Allowed Changes\\n\\n- "'`reviews/`'"\\n- "'`evidence/`'"\\n')
    for rel in files:
        f=p/rel; f.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True); f.write_text('x\\n')
    for rel,target in symlinks:
        f=p/rel; f.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True); f.symlink_to(target)
    if gitmodules is not None: (p/'.gitmodules').write_text(gitmodules)
    (rd/'reviews/case.md').write_text(f'- "'`{citation}`'"\\n')
    violations,cov=vp.verify_project(p)
    dangling=[v['detail'] for v in violations if v['kind']=='dangling-citation']
    return {'citation':citation,'dangling_count':len(dangling),'dangling':dangling,'coverage':cov}

   302	
   303	def _looks_like_placeholder(cleaned: str) -> bool:
   304	    """True if the span contains an angle-bracket placeholder anywhere.
   305	
   306	    Templated paths such as ``goals/<id>/data-contract.md`` describe a
   307	    *shape* of path, not a literal reference — the leading-character check
   308	    below only catches a span that *starts* with `<`; a placeholder in the
   309	    middle of an otherwise pathish span (e.g. after a real prefix like
   310	    `goals/`) needs its own check.
   311	    """
   312	    return "<" in cleaned or ">" in cleaned
   313	
   314	
   315	def _looks_like_out_of_project(cleaned: str) -> bool:
   316	    """True if the span is home-relative (``~/...``), POSIX filesystem-
   317	    absolute (``/...``, which also covers a UNC path once
   318	    `pathish_citations` has normalized its backslashes to slashes, e.g.
   319	    ``//server/share/...``), or Windows drive-absolute (``C:/...``).
   320	
   321	    These name a location outside the project tree, not a project-relative
   322	    citation. Rule B has no project-declared base to resolve such a path
   323	    against, so — exactly like a `scheme://` URL — it is exempt rather than
   324	    checked (and rather than mis-resolved: `Path(base) / "/abs/x"` silently
   325	    discards `base` and re-checks the literal absolute path against the real
   326	    filesystem, which is not what this rule is verifying). See the module
   327	    docstring for the known-uncovered case this leaves (e.g. an external
   328	    `~/.llm-wiki/...` design-doc store) and for `citations_exempt_external`,
   329	    the coverage field that now counts how many spans this exemption
   330	    swallowed instead of doing so silently.
   331	    """
   332	    return (
   333	        cleaned.startswith("~/")
   334	        or cleaned.startswith("/")
   335	        or bool(WINDOWS_DRIVE_ABS_RE.match(cleaned))
   336	    )
   337	
   338	
   339	def strip_locator_suffix(cleaned: str) -> str:
   340	    """Strip a trailing `::<anchor>`, `:<line>`, or `:<start>-<end>` locator.
   341	
   342	    Reviewers commonly cite a position *within* a file rather than just the
   343	    file itself: `plugins/foo/scripts/check_setup.py:123` (a line number),
   344	    `docs/x.md:10-20` (a line range), or `.hopper/tasks/foo.md::root` (a
   345	    named anchor). The locator is not part of the path — checking the
   346	    literal locator-suffixed string against the filesystem always fails
   347	    even when the file plainly exists. The `::anchor` form is tried first
   348	    since it is the more specific shape (and would otherwise be partially
   349	    consumed by the line-number pattern if the anchor itself looked
   350	    numeric). Returns `cleaned` unchanged if neither suffix is present.
   351	    """
   352	    match = ANCHOR_SUFFIX_RE.search(cleaned)
   353	    if match:
   354	        return cleaned[: match.start()]
   355	    match = LINE_SUFFIX_RE.search(cleaned)
   356	    if match:
   357	        return cleaned[: match.start()]
   358	    return cleaned
   359	
   360	
   361	def pathish_citations(markdown_text: str) -> tuple[list[str], int]:
   362	    """Extract citation spans that look like file paths.
   363	
   364	    Beyond the protocol prefixes, any slash-containing span with a file
   365	    extension, a trailing slash, or a `..` segment is treated as a path so
   366	    that citations of source/test files (e.g. `src/app.py`) are verified too.
   367	    Spans with spaces, URLs, flags, and variables are ignored, as are
   368	    regex/glob patterns (`_looks_like_pattern`), bare-domain URLs
   369	    (`_looks_like_bare_domain`), templated paths containing an
   370	    angle-bracket placeholder (`_looks_like_placeholder`, e.g.
   371	    `goals/<id>/data-contract.md`), and home-relative, POSIX-absolute, or
   372	    Windows-absolute paths naming a location outside the project
   373	    (`_looks_like_out_of_project`, e.g. `~/.llm-wiki/x.md`, `/etc/hosts`, or
   374	    `C:/Users/x`). A line carrying (or immediately following a line that
   375	    carries) the `<!-- verify:ignore -->` marker has all of its citations
   376	    skipped — see module docstring.
   377	
   378	    Returns `(cited, exempt_external)`: `cited` is the list of spans to
   379	    existence-check, and `exempt_external` is a count of spans skipped
   380	    specifically by `_looks_like_out_of_project` (not any other exemption)
   381	    — the caller accumulates this into the `citations_exempt_external`
   382	    coverage field so that exemption is visible in coverage rather than
   383	    disappearing without a trace.
   384	    """
   385	    cited: list[str] = []
   386	    exempt_external = 0
   387	    lines = markdown_text.splitlines()
   388	    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   389	        if IGNORE_MARKER in line or (i > 0 and IGNORE_MARKER in lines[i - 1]):
   390	            continue
   391	        for span in CODE_SPAN.findall(line):
   392	            cleaned = span.strip().replace("\\", "/")
   393	            if not cleaned or " " in cleaned or "://" in cleaned:
   394	                continue
   395	            if cleaned.startswith(("-", "$", "<")):
   396	                continue
   397	            if _looks_like_pattern(cleaned):
   398	                continue
   399	            if _looks_like_bare_domain(cleaned):
   400	                continue
   401	            if _looks_like_placeholder(cleaned):
   402	                continue
   403	            if _looks_like_out_of_project(cleaned):
   404	                exempt_external += 1
   405	                continue
   406	            if cleaned.startswith(PATHISH_PREFIXES):
   407	                cited.append(cleaned)
   408	                continue
   409	            if "/" in cleaned:
   410	                tail = cleaned.rsplit("/", 1)[-1]
   411	                if Path(tail).suffix or cleaned.endswith("/") or ".." in cleaned:
   412	                    cited.append(cleaned)
   413	    return cited, exempt_external
   414	
   415	
   416	def submodule_roots(project: Path) -> list[Path]:
   417	    """Every git submodule directory declared in .gitmodules, at any depth.
   418	
   419	    Used as extra resolution bases for Rule B citation existence (not for
   420	    Rule A scope-lock containment). A review may cite a path relative to a
   421	    submodule's own root (e.g. `plugins/harnessloop/` when `harnessloop` is
   422	    checked out as a submodule at <project>/harnessloop) rather than
   423	    relative to the outer project root; without this, such citations are
   424	    dangling relative to every existing base even though the file exists.
   425	
   426	    `.gitmodules` `path =` values are honored verbatim, including nested
   427	    ones (e.g. `path = kernels/openclaw`) — a submodule need not be
   428	    checked out directly under the project root. Projects without a
   429	    .gitmodules file get an empty list and behavior is unchanged.
   430	
   431	    A `path =` value is *containment-checked* before being accepted: if it
   432	    (after normalizing) does not resolve inside `project`, it is dropped
   433	    rather than followed. `.gitmodules` is ordinary tracked text a review
   434	    (or an adversarial one) could contain a `path = ../outside` entry, and
   435	    without this check a sibling directory outside the project — reachable
   436	    on the host filesystem but not part of it — would become a citation
   437	    resolution base, letting an otherwise-dangling citation resolve against
   438	    something outside the project (TH-0008 REWORK: `submodule_parent_escape`).
   439	    """
   440	    gitmodules = project / ".gitmodules"
   441	    if not gitmodules.is_file():
   442	        return []
   443	    path_re = re.compile(r"^\s*path\s*=\s*(.+?)\s*$")
   444	    roots: list[Path] = []
   445	    for line in gitmodules.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace").splitlines():
   446	        match = path_re.match(line)
   447	        if not match:
   448	            continue
   449	        rel = match.group(1).strip().replace("\\", "/")
   450	        if not rel:
   451	            continue
   452	        candidate = project / rel
   453	        normalized = Path(os.path.normpath(str(candidate)))
   454	        if not is_under(normalized, project):
   455	            continue
   456	        if candidate.is_dir():
   457	            roots.append(candidate)
   458	    return roots
   459	
   460	
   461	def _git_tracked_index(project: Path) -> dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]] | None:
   462	    """Git-tracked-file index for `build_suffix_index`, or `None` if
   463	    `project` is not itself a git working-tree root (or `git` is
   464	    unavailable).
   465	
   466	    Only engages when `project` *is* the git top level — not merely inside
   467	    one — so a scratch directory nested under some ancestor repository
   468	    (e.g. a test fixture created under this repo's own working tree without
   469	    being `git add`ed) correctly falls back to the walk-based index instead
   470	    of silently seeing zero tracked files and treating every suffix lookup
   471	    as absent.
   472	
   473	    Uses `git ls-files --recurse-submodules`, which lists tracked files in
   474	    `project` and inside every nested submodule (any depth) without ever
   475	    walking into gitignored output (`node_modules/`, `dist/`, `build/`
   476	    artifacts, `.venv/`, ...) — so the resulting uniqueness universe is the
   477	    project's real tracked-source surface, not an approximation of it via a
   478	    hardcoded noise-directory denylist (see module docstring: "unique"
   479	    means unique among git-tracked files, not literally the whole tree).
   480	    """
   481	    try:
   482	        toplevel = subprocess.run(
   483	            ["git", "-C", str(project), "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel"],
   484	            capture_output=True,
   485	            text=True,
   486	            timeout=10,
   487	        )
   488	    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   489	        return None
   490	    if toplevel.returncode != 0:
   491	        return None
   492	    top = toplevel.stdout.strip()
   493	    if not top:
   494	        return None
   495	    try:
   496	        if Path(top).resolve() != project.resolve():
   497	            return None
   498	    except OSError:
   499	        return None
   500	
   501	    try:
   502	        listed = subprocess.run(
   503	            ["git", "-C", str(project), "ls-files", "-z", "--recurse-submodules"],
   504	            capture_output=True,
   505	            timeout=60,
   506	        )
   507	    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   508	        return None
   509	    if listed.returncode != 0:
   510	        return None
   511	
   512	    index: dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]] = {}
   513	    for raw in listed.stdout.split(b"\0"):
   514	        if not raw:
   515	            continue
   516	        try:
   517	            rel = raw.decode("utf-8")
   518	        except UnicodeDecodeError:
   519	            continue
   520	        parts = tuple(p for p in rel.split("/") if p)
   521	        if not parts:
   522	            continue
   523	        index.setdefault(parts[-1], []).append(parts)
   524	    return index
   525	
   526	
   527	def build_suffix_index(project: Path) -> dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]]:
   528	    """Index files under `project` by basename, once per run.
   529	
   530	    Backs the Rule B suffix-unique fallback (`suffix_unique_match`): after a
   531	    citation fails to resolve against every explicit base (project root,
   532	    goal/round dirs, `.harnessloop/`, submodule roots — with and without a
   533	    locator suffix stripped), it is tried once more as a path *suffix*
   534	    against this index. Building it once per `verify_project` call (rather
   535	    than walking the tree per citation) keeps that fallback from turning an
   536	    O(citations) check into an O(citations * tree size) one.
   537	
   538	    Primary source is `git ls-files --recurse-submodules` (`_git_tracked_index`),
   539	    used whenever `project` is itself a git working-tree root. Falls back to
   540	    walking the filesystem directly — `os.walk` with in-place `dirnames`
   541	    pruning of `NOISE_DIR_NAMES` (`.git`, `node_modules`, build output,
   542	    venvs, ...), not `Path.rglob("*")`, since a project with vendored
   543	    dependencies can have orders of magnitude more files inside those
   544	    directories than in the rest of the tree — when `project` is not a git
   545	    working-tree root or `git` is unavailable. Each entry maps a basename to
   546	    the list of path-segment tuples (relative to `project`) of every
   547	    indexed file with that basename. Note that an indexed entry is not, by
   548	    itself, proof the file still exists: `suffix_unique_match` re-verifies
   549	    the specific matched path against the real filesystem before accepting
   550	    it (see its docstring).
   551	    """
   552	    tracked = _git_tracked_index(project)
   553	    if tracked is not None:
   554	        return tracked
   555	
   556	    index: dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]] = {}
   557	    for dirpath, dirnames, filenames in os.walk(project):
   558	        dirnames[:] = [d for d in dirnames if d not in NOISE_DIR_NAMES]
   559	        rel_dir = Path(dirpath).relative_to(project)
   560	        dir_parts = () if str(rel_dir) == "." else rel_dir.parts
   561	        for filename in filenames:
   562	            parts = dir_parts + (filename,)
   563	            index.setdefault(filename, []).append(parts)
   564	    return index
   565	
   566	
   567	def _exists_as(path: Path, want_dir: bool) -> bool:
   568	    """Existence check honoring trailing-slash directory semantics.
   569	
   570	    A citation ending in `/` names a directory: it must resolve to an
   571	    actual directory (`is_dir()`), not merely to any filesystem entry of
   572	    the same name. Without this, `pkg/real.md/` would resolve against the
   573	    *file* `pkg/real.md` via plain `exists()` (TH-0008 REWORK:
   574	    `trailing_slash_file`).
   575	    """
   576	    if want_dir:
   577	        return path.is_dir()
   578	    return path.exists()
   579	
   580	
   581	def suffix_unique_match(
   582	    cleaned: str, index: dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]], project: Path
   583	) -> bool:
   584	    """True if `cleaned` matches exactly one indexed file as a path
   585	    *suffix*, and that specific file still actually exists.
   586	
   587	    Last-resort Rule B fallback (see module docstring) for a citation that
   588	    is correct but written relative to none of the explicit bases — e.g.
   589	    `harnessloop-setup/SKILL.md` for a file that actually lives at
   590	    `plugins/harnessloop/skills/harnessloop-setup/SKILL.md`. Deliberately
   591	    conservative in four ways, each guarding a specific false-negative
   592	    risk:
   593	
   594	    - Comparison is by path *segment*, not raw string suffix — `os.html`
   595	      must not match `.../macos.html` (string `endswith` would).
   596	    - At least two path segments are required — a single bare filename
   597	      (`agents/`-style single component citations, or a bare basename like
   598	      `verify_protocol.py`) is the shape most likely to be a typo and least
   599	      likely to disambiguate anything, so it never qualifies here; it is
   600	      still checked (and can still fail) against the explicit bases above.
   601	    - The match must be *unique* across the index — 0 hits is still
   602	      dangling (no such file), and >=2 hits is also still dangling (an
   603	      ambiguous suffix is not a resolved citation; picking one of several
   604	      candidates would silently paper over which file was meant).
   605	    - The unique match's specific path is re-checked against the real
   606	      filesystem before being accepted — `is_dir()` if `cleaned` ends in
   607	      `/`, otherwise `exists()` (`_exists_as`) — rather than trusted purely
   608	      because the index once recorded it. The index can otherwise be
   609	      stale (a file the index recorded can be deleted between
   610	      `build_suffix_index` and this call — TH-0008 REWORK:
   611	      `stale_index_after_delete`) or, when it was built by walking the
   612	      filesystem, can contain a broken symlink that `os.walk` lists in
   613	      `filenames` without ever following it (TH-0008 REWORK:
   614	      `broken_symlink`); either way, treating the indexed entry as
   615	      sufficient proof of existence would accept a citation to something
   616	      that, right now, does not resolve to a real file.
   617	    """
   618	    parts = tuple(p for p in cleaned.split("/") if p)
   619	    if len(parts) < 2:
   620	        return False
   621	    candidates = index.get(parts[-1], [])
   622	    matches = [c for c in candidates if len(c) >= len(parts) and c[-len(parts) :] == parts]
   623	    if len(matches) != 1:
   624	        return False
   625	    match_path = project.joinpath(*matches[0])
   626	    return _exists_as(match_path, cleaned.endswith("/"))
   627	
   628	
   629	def _resolve_in_project(base: Path, cited: str, project: Path) -> Path | None:
   630	    """Join `base` and `cited`, normalize, and return the result only if it
   631	    stays within `project` — otherwise `None`.
   632	
   633	    Guards against a citation containing `../` segments walking the join
   634	    outside the project tree, where `Path.exists()` would silently consult
   635	    the real host filesystem for an unrelated path that happens to share a
   636	    name with something the review meant to cite (TH-0008 REWORK:
   637	    `submodule_parent_escape` — the same containment discipline
   638	    `submodule_roots` applies to `.gitmodules` entries, applied here to
   639	    every citation resolution).
   640	    """
   641	    candidate = Path(os.path.normpath(str(base / cited)))
   642	    if not is_under(candidate, project):
   643	        return None
   644	    return candidate
   645	
   646	
   647	def _any_base_resolves(cited: str, bases: list[Path], project: Path, want_dir: bool) -> bool:
   648	    """True if `cited` resolves (containment-checked, directory-semantics-
   649	    aware) against any of `bases`."""
   650	    for base in bases:
   651	        resolved = _resolve_in_project(base, cited, project)
   652	        if resolved is not None and _exists_as(resolved, want_dir):
   653	            return True
   654	    return False
   655	
   656	
   657	def verify_round(
   658	    project: Path, round_dir: Path, suffix_index: dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]]
   659	) -> tuple[list[dict], dict]:
   660	    violations: list[dict] = []
   661	    goal_dir = round_dir.parent.parent
   662	    bases = [project, goal_dir, round_dir]
   663	    # Rule B only: extra resolution bases for (1) citations that use one of
   664	    # the PATHISH_PREFIXES verbatim (setup/, state/, goals/, ...) — those
   665	    # directories actually live under <project>/.harnessloop/, which none of
   666	    # `bases` covers on their own — and (2) citations written relative to a
   667	    # submodule's own root. Kept separate from `bases` so Rule A's
   668	    # scope-lock containment check is not loosened by either addition.
   669	    citation_bases = bases + [project / ".harnessloop"] + submodule_roots(project)
   670	
   671	    checked_files = [
   672	        path
   673	        for sub in ("evidence", "reviews")
   674	        for path in sorted((round_dir / sub).rglob("*"))
   675	        if path.is_file()
   676	    ]
   677	
   678	    coverage = _empty_coverage()
   679	    coverage["rounds"] = 1
   680	    if not checked_files:
   681	        coverage["rounds_zero_inspected"] = 1
   682	
   683	    # E2(a): scope-lock existence and Allowed-Changes parseability are
   684	    # checked unconditionally for every round, independent of whether the
   685	    # round has any evidence/review files. Before this change both checks
   686	    # lived behind `if checked_files:`, so a round with nothing under
   687	    # evidence/ or reviews/ never had its scope-lock inspected at all (the
   688	    # historical "9 rounds zero-inspected, still exit 0" gap). Containment
   689	    # (below) still requires artifacts to check, so it stays guarded.
   690	    scope_lock = round_dir / "scope-lock.md"
   691	    spans: list[str] = []
   692	    if not scope_lock.exists():
   693	        violations.append(
   694	            {
   695	                "round": str(round_dir),
   696	                "kind": "missing-scope-lock",
   697	                "detail": f"{scope_lock} does not exist",
   698	            }
   699	        )
   700	    else:
   701	        spans = extract_allowed_spans(scope_lock.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
   702	        if not spans:
   703	            violations.append(
   704	                {
   705	                    "round": str(round_dir),
   706	                    "kind": "unparseable-allowed-changes",
   707	                    "detail": f"no backtick path spans found under '## Allowed Changes' in {scope_lock}",
   708	                }
   709	            )
   710	
   711	    if checked_files and spans:
   712	        coverage["rule_a_files"] = len(checked_files)
   713	        for file_path in checked_files:
   714	            allowed = any(
   715	                is_under(file_path, base / span)
   716	                for base in bases
   717	                for span in spans
   718	            )
   719	            if not allowed:
   720	                violations.append(
   721	                    {
   722	                        "round": str(round_dir),
   723	                        "kind": "scope-lock-violation",
   724	                        "detail": f"{file_path} is outside every allowed path in {scope_lock}",
   725	                    }
   726	                )
   727	
   728	    reviews_dir = round_dir / "reviews"
   729	    if reviews_dir.is_dir():
   730	        for review in sorted(reviews_dir.rglob("*.md")):
   731	            coverage["rule_b_files"] += 1
   732	            cited_list, exempt_external = pathish_citations(review.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
   733	            coverage["citations_exempt_external"] += exempt_external
   734	            for cited in cited_list:
   735	                coverage["citations_checked"] += 1
   736	                want_dir = cited.endswith("/")
   737	                resolved = _any_base_resolves(cited, citation_bases, project, want_dir)
   738	                stripped = cited
   739	                if not resolved:
   740	                    stripped = strip_locator_suffix(cited)
   741	                    if stripped != cited:
   742	                        resolved = _any_base_resolves(stripped, citation_bases, project, want_dir)
   743	                if not resolved:
   744	                    resolved = suffix_unique_match(stripped, suffix_index, project)
   745	                if not resolved:
   746	                    violations.append(
   747	                        {
   748	                            "round": str(round_dir),
   749	                            "kind": "dangling-citation",
   750	                            "detail": f"{review} cites `{cited}` which does not exist",
   751	                        }
   752	                    )
   753	
   754	    # E4: same-file enum contradiction in decision.md. Deliberately the
   755	    # narrowest possible check — two enum lines in one file, compared after
   756	    # strip().lower(). No prose parsing, no path resolution, no cross-file
   757	    # join, no value normalization: those are exactly what produced six of
   758	    # this repo's ten evolution issues. Absent fields are never a violation,
   759	    # so existing rounds need no migration.
   760	    decision = round_dir / "decision.md"
   761	    if decision.exists():
   762	        verdict = residuals = None
   763	        for line in decision.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="ignore").splitlines():
   764	            stripped = line.strip()
   765	            if verdict is None and stripped.lower().startswith("- verdict:"):
   766	                verdict = stripped.split(":", 1)[1].strip().lower()
   767	            elif residuals is None and stripped.lower().startswith("- residuals:"):
   768	                residuals = stripped.split(":", 1)[1].strip().lower()
   769	        if verdict == "pass" and residuals not in (None, "", "none"):
   770	            violations.append(
   771	                {
   772	                    "round": str(round_dir),
   773	                    "kind": "verdict-residual-contradiction",
   774	                    "detail": (
   775	                        f"{decision} declares `Verdict: pass` while `Residuals` is "
   776	                        f"non-none — use `pass-with-residual` when part of the claim "
   777	                        f"is uncovered or deferred"
   778	                    ),
   779	                }
   780	            )
   781	
   782	    return violations, coverage
   783	
   784	
   785	def _empty_coverage() -> dict:
   786	    """Zeroed coverage accumulator. Keys match the `### Mechanical Gate
   787	    Boundary` IN column in harnessloop-loop/SKILL.md one-to-one; a rename on
   788	    either side must update the other."""
   789	    return {
   790	        "rounds": 0,
   791	        "rounds_zero_inspected": 0,
   792	        "rule_a_files": 0,
   793	        "rule_b_files": 0,
   794	        "citations_checked": 0,
   795	        "citations_exempt_external": 0,
   796	    }
   797	
   798	
   799	def verify_project(project: Path) -> tuple[list[dict], dict]:
   800	    goals_dir = project / ".harnessloop" / "goals"
   801	    coverage = _empty_coverage()
   802	    if not goals_dir.is_dir():
   803	        return [], coverage
   804	    violations: list[dict] = []
   805	    # Built once per project run (not per round/citation) — see
   806	    # `build_suffix_index` for why this matters for performance.
   807	    suffix_index = build_suffix_index(project)
   808	    for round_dir in sorted(goals_dir.glob("*/rounds/*")):
   809	        if round_dir.is_dir():
   810	            round_violations, round_coverage = verify_round(project, round_dir, suffix_index)
   811	            violations.extend(round_violations)
   812	            for key in coverage:
   813	                coverage[key] += round_coverage[key]
   814	    return violations, coverage
   815	
   816	
   817	def main() -> int:
   818	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
   819	        description=(
   820	            "Verify mechanical Harnessloop protocol gates (scope-lock containment "
   821	            "and citation existence). Citation spans that are regex/glob patterns, "
   822	            "bare-domain URLs (docs.python.org/..., github.com/...), or templated "
   823	            "paths with an angle-bracket placeholder (goals/<id>/...) are exempt "
   824	            "automatically; a citation known to be intentional prose rather than a "
   825	            "real reference can be exempted explicitly by putting "
   826	            "'<!-- verify:ignore -->' on the same line or the line before it. "
   827	            "Citations are resolved against the project root, the round's goal and "
   828	            "round directories, the project's own .harnessloop/ directory (for "
   829	            "citations using a PATHISH_PREFIXES prefix verbatim, e.g. "
   830	            "setup/data-sources.md), and the root of every git submodule (any depth) "
   831	            "declared in the project's .gitmodules (containment-checked: a path or "
   832	            ".gitmodules entry that would resolve outside the project is never treated "
   833	            "as resolved). A trailing :<line>, :<start>-<end>, or ::<anchor> locator is "
   834	            "stripped before checking. If still unresolved, a citation with >=2 path "
   835	            "segments that matches exactly one file in the project's git-tracked index "
   836	            "(or, outside a git working tree, the walked and noise-pruned tree) as a "
   837	            "path suffix, and whose matched path still actually exists, is also "
   838	            "accepted. A citation ending in / must resolve to a directory. Home-relative "
   839	            "(~/...), filesystem-absolute (/...), and Windows-absolute (C:/..., "
   840	            "\\\\server\\share...) citations are exempt (out of project scope; counted "
   841	            "in the citations_exempt_external coverage field)."
   842	        )
   843	    )
   844	    parser.add_argument("--project", "-p", default=".", help="Target project directory. Defaults to current directory.")
   845	    parser.add_argument("--json", action="store_true", help="Print machine-readable JSON output.")
   846	    args = parser.parse_args()
   847	
   848	    project = Path(args.project).resolve()
   849	    if not project.is_dir():
   850	        print(f"Project directory not found: {project}", file=sys.stderr)
   851	        return 2
   852	
   853	    violations, coverage = verify_project(project)
   854	    if args.json:
   855	        print(
   856	            json.dumps(
   857	                {"project": str(project), "violations": violations, "coverage": coverage},
   858	                indent=2,
   859	                ensure_ascii=False,
   860	            )
   861	        )
   862	    else:
   863	        print(f"Harnessloop verify: {project}")
   864	        if violations:
   865	            for violation in violations:
   866	                print(f"  [{violation['kind']}] {violation['detail']}")
   867	            print(f"{len(violations)} violation(s) found.")
   868	        elif coverage["rounds_zero_inspected"] > 0:
   869	            # E2(b): a clean exit is not a blanket "all clear" when some
   870	            # rounds had nothing under evidence/ or reviews/ to check in the
   871	            # first place. Print the qualified form instead of the
   872	            # unqualified banner below so a clean exit for those rounds
   873	            # cannot be misread as "checked and clean".
   874	            print(
   875	                "passed, but not a clean sweep: "
   876	                f"{coverage['rounds_zero_inspected']} of {coverage['rounds']} round(s) had "
   877	                "nothing under evidence/ or reviews/ to inspect — a clean exit for those "
   878	                'rounds means "nothing to check", not "checked and clean".'
   879	            )
   880	        else:
   881	            print("All mechanical protocol gates passed.")
   882	        # Unconditional coverage line (E2(b)): printed regardless of pass/fail
   883	        # so "was this gate actually run over anything" is always visible,
   884	        # not just inferable from exit code. Field names here are the short
   885	        # print-form of the `coverage` dict keys (see `_empty_coverage`);
   886	        # the --json output uses the full dict verbatim.
   887	        print(
   888	            "coverage: "
   889	            f"rounds={coverage['rounds']} rule_a_files={coverage['rule_a_files']} "
   890	            f"rule_b_files={coverage['rule_b_files']} citations={coverage['citations_checked']} "
   891	            f"citations_exempt_external={coverage['citations_exempt_external']} "
   892	            f"zero_inspected={coverage['rounds_zero_inspected']}"
   893	        )
   894	    return 1 if violations else 0
   895	
   896	
   897	if __name__ == "__main__":
   898	    raise SystemExit(main())
   438	- Acceptance of a round after a failed adversarial review.
   439	
   440	## Verification Phase
   441	
   442	Two gates operate at different layers, and both must pass before a round is accepted. `<skill-dir>/scripts/verify_protocol.py` is a mechanical gate: it enforces only machine-checkable rules (scope-lock containment, dangling evidence citations) and is run in Loop Continuation step 1. Adversarial review below is a separate model-judgment gate: it checks whether the evidence actually supports the claim. A mechanical pass is not a protocol pass — a round that exits `verify_protocol.py` clean still fails if adversarial review, thresholds, or feedback classification are not satisfied.
   443	
   444	### Mechanical Gate Boundary
   445	
   446	The mechanical gate's exit code decides less than it looks like it decides. What it currently checks (**IN** — field names below match `verify_protocol.py`'s `coverage` object, printed on every run and included under the `coverage` key of `--json` output, one-to-one):
   447	
   448	- `rounds` / `rounds_zero_inspected` — scope-lock existence (`missing-scope-lock`) and Allowed Changes parseability (`unparseable-allowed-changes`), checked unconditionally for every round regardless of whether that round has any evidence/review artifacts.
   449	- `rule_a_files` — whether files under a round's `evidence/` and `reviews/` fall inside a path the round's scope-lock allows (`scope-lock-violation`), checked only when the round has at least one such file and its scope-lock parses.
   450	- `rule_b_files` / `citations_checked` — whether backtick path-ish references inside that round's `reviews/*.md` resolve to files that exist (`dangling-citation`).
   451	- `citations_exempt_external` — how many of those citation spans were home-relative (`~/...`), filesystem-absolute (`/...`), or Windows-absolute (`C:/...`, UNC) and so were exempted from existence checking entirely (a real, uncovered gap — see `verify_protocol.py`'s module docstring — made visible in coverage rather than silently disappearing).
   452	
   453	What it does **not** decide (**OUT** — currently not decided by the mechanical gate, not "unmechanizable forever"):
   454	
   455	- Whether the cited evidence actually supports the round's conclusion.
   456	- Whether a stated threshold was met.
   457	- Whether a test genuinely asserts anything, versus asserting nothing and passing vacuously.
   458	- Whether `pass` / `positive` wording is honest about what happened.
   459	- Whether a business-code change stayed inside scope.
   460	
   461	Coverage fact: Rule A only inspects `evidence/` and `reviews/`; Rule B only inspects that round's `reviews/*.md`. Neither rule reads the business-code change itself, and neither reads `.harnessloop/state/`. **An exit 0 must not be read as "every artifact this round produced was inspected"** — only that the files under those two directories were. A round with nothing under `evidence/` or `reviews/` still exits 0 and is counted in `rounds_zero_inspected`, which means "nothing to check", not "checked and clean".
   462	
   463	Discipline: when the mechanical gate fails, do not make it pass by editing the checked artifact unless that artifact is genuinely wrong — the correct fix is `$harnessloop-issue` plus an explicit, recorded exemption. If a round's `scope-lock.md` is edited after that round's `evidence/` already exists, `decision.md` must record one line explaining why.
   464	
   465	Do not accept a round with a generic engineering review alone.
   466	
   467	Assign adversarial review that checks the work against:
   468	
   469	- Real static data freshness.
   470	- Dynamic/generated data behavior.

   460	
   461	def _git_tracked_index(project: Path) -> dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]] | None:
   462	    """Git-tracked-file index for `build_suffix_index`, or `None` if
   463	    `project` is not itself a git working-tree root (or `git` is
   464	    unavailable).
   465	
   466	    Only engages when `project` *is* the git top level — not merely inside
   467	    one — so a scratch directory nested under some ancestor repository
   468	    (e.g. a test fixture created under this repo's own working tree without
   469	    being `git add`ed) correctly falls back to the walk-based index instead
   470	    of silently seeing zero tracked files and treating every suffix lookup
   471	    as absent.
   472	
   473	    Uses `git ls-files --recurse-submodules`, which lists tracked files in
   474	    `project` and inside every nested submodule (any depth) without ever
   475	    walking into gitignored output (`node_modules/`, `dist/`, `build/`
   476	    artifacts, `.venv/`, ...) — so the resulting uniqueness universe is the
   477	    project's real tracked-source surface, not an approximation of it via a
   478	    hardcoded noise-directory denylist (see module docstring: "unique"
   479	    means unique among git-tracked files, not literally the whole tree).
   480	    """
   481	    try:
   482	        toplevel = subprocess.run(
   483	            ["git", "-C", str(project), "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel"],
   484	            capture_output=True,
   485	            text=True,
   486	            timeout=10,
   487	        )
   488	    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   489	        return None
   490	    if toplevel.returncode != 0:
   491	        return None
   492	    top = toplevel.stdout.strip()
   493	    if not top:
   494	        return None
   495	    try:
   496	        if Path(top).resolve() != project.resolve():
   497	            return None
   498	    except OSError:
   499	        return None
   500	
   501	    try:
   502	        listed = subprocess.run(
   503	            ["git", "-C", str(project), "ls-files", "-z", "--recurse-submodules"],
   504	            capture_output=True,
   505	            timeout=60,
   506	        )
   507	    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   508	        return None
   509	    if listed.returncode != 0:
   510	        return None
   511	
   512	    index: dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]] = {}
   513	    for raw in listed.stdout.split(b"\0"):
   514	        if not raw:
   515	            continue
   516	        try:
   517	            rel = raw.decode("utf-8")
   518	        except UnicodeDecodeError:
   519	            continue
   520	        parts = tuple(p for p in rel.split("/") if p)
   521	        if not parts:
   522	            continue
   523	        index.setdefault(parts[-1], []).append(parts)
   524	    return index
   525	
   526	
   527	def build_suffix_index(project: Path) -> dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]]:
   528	    """Index files under `project` by basename, once per run.
   529	
   530	    Backs the Rule B suffix-unique fallback (`suffix_unique_match`): after a
   531	    citation fails to resolve against every explicit base (project root,
   532	    goal/round dirs, `.harnessloop/`, submodule roots — with and without a
   533	    locator suffix stripped), it is tried once more as a path *suffix*
   534	    against this index. Building it once per `verify_project` call (rather
   535	    than walking the tree per citation) keeps that fallback from turning an
   536	    O(citations) check into an O(citations * tree size) one.
   537	
   538	    Primary source is `git ls-files --recurse-submodules` (`_git_tracked_index`),
   539	    used whenever `project` is itself a git working-tree root. Falls back to
   540	    walking the filesystem directly — `os.walk` with in-place `dirnames`
   541	    pruning of `NOISE_DIR_NAMES` (`.git`, `node_modules`, build output,
   542	    venvs, ...), not `Path.rglob("*")`, since a project with vendored
   543	    dependencies can have orders of magnitude more files inside those
   544	    directories than in the rest of the tree — when `project` is not a git
   545	    working-tree root or `git` is unavailable. Each entry maps a basename to
   546	    the list of path-segment tuples (relative to `project`) of every
   547	    indexed file with that basename. Note that an indexed entry is not, by
   548	    itself, proof the file still exists: `suffix_unique_match` re-verifies
   549	    the specific matched path against the real filesystem before accepting
   550	    it (see its docstring).
   551	    """
   552	    tracked = _git_tracked_index(project)
   553	    if tracked is not None:
   554	        return tracked
   555	
   556	    index: dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]] = {}
   557	    for dirpath, dirnames, filenames in os.walk(project):
   558	        dirnames[:] = [d for d in dirnames if d not in NOISE_DIR_NAMES]
   559	        rel_dir = Path(dirpath).relative_to(project)
   560	        dir_parts = () if str(rel_dir) == "." else rel_dir.parts
   561	        for filename in filenames:
   562	            parts = dir_parts + (filename,)
   563	            index.setdefault(filename, []).append(parts)
   564	    return index
   565	
   566	
   567	def _exists_as(path: Path, want_dir: bool) -> bool:
   568	    """Existence check honoring trailing-slash directory semantics.
   569	
   570	    A citation ending in `/` names a directory: it must resolve to an
   571	    actual directory (`is_dir()`), not merely to any filesystem entry of
   572	    the same name. Without this, `pkg/real.md/` would resolve against the
   573	    *file* `pkg/real.md` via plain `exists()` (TH-0008 REWORK:
   574	    `trailing_slash_file`).
   575	    """
   576	    if want_dir:
   577	        return path.is_dir()
   578	    return path.exists()
   579	
   580	
   581	def suffix_unique_match(
   582	    cleaned: str, index: dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]], project: Path
   583	) -> bool:
   584	    """True if `cleaned` matches exactly one indexed file as a path
   585	    *suffix*, and that specific file still actually exists.
   586	
   587	    Last-resort Rule B fallback (see module docstring) for a citation that
   588	    is correct but written relative to none of the explicit bases — e.g.
   589	    `harnessloop-setup/SKILL.md` for a file that actually lives at
   590	    `plugins/harnessloop/skills/harnessloop-setup/SKILL.md`. Deliberately
   591	    conservative in four ways, each guarding a specific false-negative
   592	    risk:
   593	
   594	    - Comparison is by path *segment*, not raw string suffix — `os.html`
   595	      must not match `.../macos.html` (string `endswith` would).
   596	    - At least two path segments are required — a single bare filename
   597	      (`agents/`-style single component citations, or a bare basename like
   598	      `verify_protocol.py`) is the shape most likely to be a typo and least
   599	      likely to disambiguate anything, so it never qualifies here; it is
   600	      still checked (and can still fail) against the explicit bases above.
   601	    - The match must be *unique* across the index — 0 hits is still
   602	      dangling (no such file), and >=2 hits is also still dangling (an
   603	      ambiguous suffix is not a resolved citation; picking one of several
   604	      candidates would silently paper over which file was meant).
   605	    - The unique match's specific path is re-checked against the real
   606	      filesystem before being accepted — `is_dir()` if `cleaned` ends in
   607	      `/`, otherwise `exists()` (`_exists_as`) — rather than trusted purely
   608	      because the index once recorded it. The index can otherwise be
   609	      stale (a file the index recorded can be deleted between
   610	      `build_suffix_index` and this call — TH-0008 REWORK:
   611	      `stale_index_after_delete`) or, when it was built by walking the
   612	      filesystem, can contain a broken symlink that `os.walk` lists in
   613	      `filenames` without ever following it (TH-0008 REWORK:
   614	      `broken_symlink`); either way, treating the indexed entry as
   615	      sufficient proof of existence would accept a citation to something
   616	      that, right now, does not resolve to a real file.
   617	    """
   618	    parts = tuple(p for p in cleaned.split("/") if p)
   619	    if len(parts) < 2:
   620	        return False
   621	    candidates = index.get(parts[-1], [])
   622	    matches = [c for c in candidates if len(c) >= len(parts) and c[-len(parts) :] == parts]
   623	    if len(matches) != 1:
   624	        return False
   625	    match_path = project.joinpath(*matches[0])
   626	    return _exists_as(match_path, cleaned.endswith("/"))
   627	
   628	
   629	def _resolve_in_project(base: Path, cited: str, project: Path) -> Path | None:
   630	    """Join `base` and `cited`, normalize, and return the result only if it
   631	    stays within `project` — otherwise `None`.
   632	
   633	    Guards against a citation containing `../` segments walking the join
   634	    outside the project tree, where `Path.exists()` would silently consult
   635	    the real host filesystem for an unrelated path that happens to share a
   636	    name with something the review meant to cite (TH-0008 REWORK:
   637	    `submodule_parent_escape` — the same containment discipline
   638	    `submodule_roots` applies to `.gitmodules` entries, applied here to
   639	    every citation resolution).
   640	    """
   641	    candidate = Path(os.path.normpath(str(base / cited)))
   642	    if not is_under(candidate, project):
   643	        return None
   644	    return candidate
   645	
   646	
   647	def _any_base_resolves(cited: str, bases: list[Path], project: Path, want_dir: bool) -> bool:
   648	    """True if `cited` resolves (containment-checked, directory-semantics-
   649	    aware) against any of `bases`."""
   650	    for base in bases:
   651	        resolved = _resolve_in_project(base, cited, project)
   652	        if resolved is not None and _exists_as(resolved, want_dir):
   653	            return True
   654	    return False
   655	
   656	
   657	def verify_round(
   658	    project: Path, round_dir: Path, suffix_index: dict[str, list[tuple[str, ...]]]
   659	) -> tuple[list[dict], dict]:
   660	    violations: list[dict] = []
   900	            "suffix_unique_match accepts a unique multi-segment suffix hit (TH-0008 original case)",
   901	        )
   902	        check(
   903	            verify_protocol.suffix_unique_match("SKILL.md", unique_index, suffix_root) is False,
   904	            "suffix_unique_match rejects a single (bare) segment even when the basename is indexed (false-negative guard: typo-prone, no context)",
   905	        )
   906	        check(
   907	            verify_protocol.suffix_unique_match("harnessloop-setup/SKIL.md", unique_index, suffix_root) is False,
   908	            "suffix_unique_match rejects a typo'd basename with zero index hits (false-negative guard)",
   909	        )
   910	        check(
   911	            verify_protocol.suffix_unique_match("component/os.html", unique_index, suffix_root) is False,
   912	            "suffix_unique_match does not let `os.html` match `.../macos.html` (segment comparison, not string endswith)",
   913	        )
   914	        check(
   915	            verify_protocol.suffix_unique_match("guide/setup.md", unique_index, suffix_root) is False,
   916	            "suffix_unique_match rejects an ambiguous suffix matching >=2 real files (false-negative guard: no false exemption from ambiguity)",
   917	        )
   918	
   919	        # TH-0008 REWORK counterexample 1/5: trailing_slash_file. A citation ending in
   920	        # `/` names a directory; `pkg/real.md/` must not resolve against the *file*
   921	        # `pkg/real.md` merely because the trailing slash was discarded by segment-splitting.
   922	        # codex T-062 repro: dangling_count=0 pre-fix; must be >=1 (i.e. no match) post-fix.
   923	        check(
   924	            verify_protocol.suffix_unique_match("pkg/real.md/", unique_index, suffix_root) is False,
   925	            "suffix_unique_match honors trailing-slash directory semantics: a file must not "
   926	            "satisfy a directory-shaped citation (TH-0008 REWORK: trailing_slash_file, "
   927	            "codex T-062)",
   928	        )
   929	        # Mutation check: with the trailing-slash guard removed (i.e. calling
   930	        # `_exists_as` unconditionally with want_dir=False, as the pre-REWORK code did),
   931	        # this same citation *does* resolve -- confirming the assertion above has teeth.
   932	        check(
   933	            verify_protocol._exists_as(suffix_root / "deep" / "pkg" / "real.md", False) is True,
   934	            "mutation control: without directory-semantics enforcement the same path would "
   935	            "wrongly resolve (proves the guard above is load-bearing, not vacuous)",
   936	        )
   937	
   938	        # TH-0008 REWORK counterexample 2/5: stale_index_after_delete. Build the index,
   939	        # delete the file it recorded, then confirm suffix_unique_match no longer trusts
   940	        # the (now stale) index entry. codex T-062 repro: match_after_delete=True,
   941	        # target_exists=False pre-fix; must be False post-fix.
   942	        gone = suffix_root / "deep" / "pkg" / "gone.md"
   943	        gone.write_text("will be deleted\n", encoding="utf-8")
   944	        stale_index = verify_protocol.build_suffix_index(suffix_root)
   945	        check(
   946	            verify_protocol.suffix_unique_match("pkg/gone.md", stale_index, suffix_root) is True,
   947	            "sanity: suffix_unique_match matches pkg/gone.md while it still exists",
   948	        )
   949	        gone.unlink()
   950	        check(
   951	            verify_protocol.suffix_unique_match("pkg/gone.md", stale_index, suffix_root) is False,
   952	            "suffix_unique_match re-verifies existence at match time and rejects a stale "
   953	            "index entry for a since-deleted file (TH-0008 REWORK: stale_index_after_delete, "
   954	            "codex T-062)",
   955	        )
   956	        # Mutation check: the index itself still (wrongly, if untrusted) records the
   957	        # deleted file as if it were real -- proving the re-verification, not index
   958	        # rebuilding, is what makes the assertion above pass.
   959	        check(
   960	            ("pkg", "gone.md")[-1] in stale_index and any(
   961	                c[-2:] == ("pkg", "gone.md") for c in stale_index.get("gone.md", [])
   962	            ),
   963	            "mutation control: the stale index still lists the deleted file (proves "
   964	            "suffix_unique_match's own re-check, not a rebuilt index, catches the staleness)",
   965	        )
   966	
   967	        # TH-0008 REWORK counterexample 3/5: broken_symlink. os.walk() lists a symlink in
   968	        # `filenames` regardless of whether its target exists; without a match-time
   969	        # existence check a broken symlink is indexed exactly like a real file. codex
   970	        # T-062 repro: dangling_count=0 pre-fix; must be >=1 post-fix. This exercises the
   971	        # walk-based fallback specifically, so it is built and indexed on a non-git
   972	        # directory (a nested subdirectory of this fixture, still not its own git
   973	        # toplevel, so build_suffix_index falls back to the walk).
   974	        try:
   975	            (suffix_root / "deep" / "pkg" / "broken.md").symlink_to("missing-target.md")
   976	            symlinks_supported = True
   977	        except (OSError, NotImplementedError):
   978	            symlinks_supported = False
   979	        if symlinks_supported:
   980	            symlink_index = verify_protocol.build_suffix_index(suffix_root)
   981	            check(
   982	                verify_protocol.suffix_unique_match("pkg/broken.md", symlink_index, suffix_root) is False,
   983	                "suffix_unique_match re-verifies existence at match time and rejects a "
   984	                "broken symlink that os.walk indexed without following "
   985	                "(TH-0008 REWORK: broken_symlink, codex T-062)",
   986	            )
   987	            # Mutation check: the symlink is genuinely indexed (walk-based index has an
   988	            # entry for it) -- proving the rejection above comes from the match-time
   989	            # existence re-check, not from the symlink being absent from the index.
   990	            check(
   991	                any(c[-2:] == ("pkg", "broken.md") for c in symlink_index.get("broken.md", [])),
   992	                "mutation control: os.walk indexes the broken symlink like a real file "
   993	                "(proves the rejection above is the match-time re-check doing the work)",
   994	            )
   995	        else:
   996	            print("  (skipped: symlinks unsupported on this filesystem -- broken_symlink counterexample)")
   997	    finally:
   998	        shutil.rmtree(suffix_root, ignore_errors=True)
   999	
  1000	    # -- _looks_like_out_of_project / pathish_citations: ~/, POSIX-absolute, and
  1001	    # Windows-absolute exemption, now counted via citations_exempt_external --
  1002	    home_and_abs_text = (
  1003	        "External design wiki, home-relative, out of project scope:\n"
  1004	        "- `~/.llm-wiki/agent-app-design/kernel/kernel-ecosystem-facts.md`\n"
  1005	        "\n"

  1019	    check(

  1030	    # -- end-to-end round fixture: locators, nested submodule, suffix fallback, ~//abs exemption --

  1032	    round_dir4 = th0008_root / ".harnessloop" / "goals" / "20260105-001-fixture" / "rounds" / "0001"

  1139	    # TH-0008 REWORK counterexample 4/5: noise_pruned_ambiguity. Two real files
  1140	    # share the cited suffix's last two segments -- one under an ordinary source

  1146	    # dangling_count=0 pre-fix; must be >=1 post-fix (git-tracked index sees both).

  1174	        git_available = shutil.which("git") is not None
  1175	        if git_available:
  1176	            init = subprocess.run(["git", "init", "-q"], cwd=noise_root, capture_output=True)
  1177	            add = subprocess.run(["git", "add", "-A"], cwd=noise_root, capture_output=True)
  1178	            git_available = init.returncode == 0 and add.returncode == 0
  1179

  1183	                any("pkg/real.md" in v["detail"] for v in violations),

  1187	            )
